The question is addressed to the Minister of Environment.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u4	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In many places there are industrial plants in Poland, which cause huge odour and odor nuisance. More and more people neighbouring such sites are demanding decisive action to eliminate such nuisances. A similar situation is taking place in my town, in Jaworzno, where in the Szczakow district there is a tannery plant, the Szczakow Garbarnia, which deals with the processing, processing of animal skins. There, the production process brings out a huge stench, a huge stench that is felt even at a long distance from the plant, especially on hot and warm days. The inhabitants have already come to an end and protest against such neighborhoods and demand decisive action. Therefore, Minister, I think that it is time that, in general, in Poland, there is a time to say, to signal such entrepreneurs that they need to think about changing technologies, so as not to reduce, but to eliminate such nuisances, or they will have to change their place of business to those where it will not disturb the inhabitants. Therefore, I have a question to the Minister: At what stage are the works related to the anti-odore law, a law which will limit such odor-distorting burdens? What legal instruments will be enshrined in this law, from which local governments or other environmental institutions will be able to benefit in order to effectively counter this kind of phenomenon? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u5	Thank you very much. The answer will be given by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, Minister Sławomir Mazurek.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u6	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, please. Scent pressure is a serious problem both on a local scale and on a national scale. This is a state of subjective discomfort felt by man, both in the physical and mental sphere, caused by the smell of substances entering the air. Fragrance is the result of the effects of sources that emit odor compounds that are recognized by humans. Ladies and gentlemen! In 2017, almost 1.5 thousand were received by inspection bodies. complaints in relation to smelling nuisance, which is 64% air pollution issues. So this is a powerful scale, and very often it concerns not only a tannery, which, of course, we will look very carefully at, and I will inform you of the results of the inspection, and the High House. About those... Subjectivity is that very often complaints concern not only tanneries, but also places that - you can say - secrete certain odors and this is part of a technological process or a poorly conducted technological process. We are talking about landfills here, we are talking about work on the processes that are subject to waste. But this often applies to places like this... If someone lives in the neighborhood of a coffee shop, after a while this coffee also becomes cumbersome, and even, ladies and gentlemen, the production of sweets can cause discomfort, it also causes complaints. Thus, in order to help businesses in these different areas to properly hermeticise their activities, a codex was prepared to counter odor nuisance. There are described best practices of using biofilters, the use of various tools that entrepreneurs can use. In the first place, we used such encouragement. Of course, we work all the time. If there are opportunities for other legislation, not only environmental legislation, to provide information on whether or not to introduce certain tools to combat odor-deprivedness, then we try to do so. The code that I told you about was already prepared in 2016. It is available on our websites, it has been widely distributed and entrepreneurs use it. Also in the Ministry of the Environment, a list of substances and chemicals has been drawn up, which cause odour nuisance. This is where expertise has been carried out in order to draw up a list of potentially odour-deprived substances, as well as to designate fragrance units and limit values for air concentrations of substances, chemical compounds, in relation to waste treatment, treatment, landfilling, agricultural production, chemical industry. So what can be measured is what we've been trying to measure, and what we've done is we've been trying to integrate it into further legislative work. Of course, with regard to the current national legislation, we have a number of regulations and environmental law, and the Fertilisation and Fertilisation Act, and the Minister's regulations on nitrogen drain from agricultural sources. There are also records in the Construction Law that help to make better quality decisions and prevent nuisance. Of course, it is also about infrastructure ministers, the economy. There are a number of these regulations. In answer to this specific question, what our current work in the environmental ministry looks like, I would like to say that here, in cooperation with the Chief Environmental Inspector, we are working with the General Directorate for Environment on a draft law on the prevention of scent nuisance, which is so difficult that there is no European regulation to which we can appeal directly. We need to adjust it to the conditions. There are sometimes regulations of a land-based nature, and we have included these examples in our analytical work. In the first place, which seems to be the most objective, and also mentioned by the Member, it is a matter of identifying the location of the planned projects, the operation of which may involve the risk of creating scent nuisance, at a safe distance from the buildings. It is considered to create, at the second stage of the work, a legal regulation that will allow an assessment of the odour nuisance of existing facilities. Of course, much depends on how well the local government works, how spatial planning plans are prepared, how public consultation looks like, when such installations are being prepared and planned. And that's also the important role of local governments. And with regard to what is on the inspection side, surely in those places that will be indicated, our inspectors will be present and will carry out an inspection of the state of the installation, of everything that may affect the odour nuisance. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u39	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! I have a question about the schedule. Is the process of implementing the power market in Poland running on a timely basis and can all interested people sleep peacefully on this issue? Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u40	Thank you. Mr Tadeusz Skobel corresponds to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Energy.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u41	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The power market provides a stable supply of electricity to households and industry in the long term, so that any energy customer in the country will be able to benefit from electricity at the time and in the amount that it needs. It is also the most economically efficient way to ensure adequate capacity, as the contractual power is contracted through an auction in which individual power providers compete with each other. This year is a special year because there will be as many as three general auctions. In the following years only one main auction will take place per year. From 2020 onwards, we will start organising additional auctions in order to get closer to the delivery period, possibly to buy the remaining power. The first power auction will take place on 15 November this year, and the power contracts concluded on it are intended to ensure continuous and stable supply of electricity in Poland in 2021. The planned purchase of capacity in this auction is 22 732 MW. The second power auction will take place on 5 December this year. and is to ensure stable deliveries in 2022. The last auction this year will take place on December 21st. Contracts will be made on it for 2023. The Power Market Act entered into force on 18 January this year, and the first power auction in Poland, in accordance with the transitional provisions, is due to take place on 15 November 2018. Someone might say that there were 10 months to prepare for the auction, but de facto the first auction is preceded by a series of actions, and there are legal acts that I will briefly discuss. In February and March this year. intensive work on the power market rules, which were subject to public consultation before approval by the President of the URE in agreement with the Minister of Energy. The draft rules of procedure were drawn up by the transmission system operator, which received nearly 900 comments. The role of the President of the URE and the Minister for Energy was to work out the text of the Rules of Procedure in a short time. The power market rules were approved on 30 March 2018. At the same time, the transmission system operator worked on the IT system, the power market register by which it carried out the general certification. Between 3 April and 29 May, the general certification of manufacturing units located in the country continued. The certification was carried out by the transmission system operator. Overall certification passed 1167 units with total net power 47,000. MW. It is worth noting that nearly 9 thousand. MW has been announced as planned and planned units. During that time, the Ministry of Energy worked intensively on three implementing acts to the Power Market Act. The result of this work was the adoption of several regulations. One of them is the Regulation of the Minister of Energy of 18 July 2018 on the performance of the Capacity Obligation, its clearing and demonstration and the conclusion of secondary market transactions. This Regulation sets out the times for which the transmission system operator may announce a period of risk, the minimum capacity that may be traded on the secondary market and the unit level of penalties for power providers who, at the request of the operator, do not deliver the contractual power. The following is the Regulation of the Minister for Energy of 22 August 2018 on the main auction parameters for the delivery periods from 2021 to 2023. It is worth noting that an independent team of experts composed of the best professors and doctors of national technical universities participated in the work on the parameters of the auction. This regulation will be issued by the Minister of Energy annually before each power auction. Based on the parameters set out in this Regulation, the operator will conduct capacity auctions. A further regulation is a regulation of the Minister of Energy of 3 September 2018 on financial security provided by power providers and participants in preliminary auctions. In order to eliminate the situation that the price will be determined by capacity market units which will in fact never arise, financial collateral has been used in capacity auctions. This Regulation specifies the authorised forms of security and the unit amount of the security to be transferred to 1 MW of contracted power. Currently, in the whole process, in terms of the power market, we are at the certification stage for the main auction. This is the second type of certification provided for in the Capacity Market Act. This certification is voluntary as opposed to general certification. During certification, power market units are created for auctions and power providers make the final decision on the size of the capacity they will report in the auction. Certification started on 5 September this year. And it will last until the end of October. Also, by the end of October, the transmission system operator is required to set up an IT system to conduct auctions and inform the Minister of Energy and the President of the Energy Regulatory Office, as both the auction and other processes in the power market are conducted electronically. Given that all the legal documents needed to carry out the first capacity auction have been issued and that the general certification has been successfully carried out and that the certification for the auction is currently ongoing, the Ministry of Energy does not see a risk to the timely [[♪ The bell ♪]] carrying out the first power auctions in Poland. There is therefore a good chance that the national electricity consumers will gain continuous and stable capacity thanks to the power market...
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u42	Finish up, Minister. Undersecretary of State of the Ministry of Energy Tadeusz Skobel: ...electricity supply in the long-term horizon. Thank you very much. Thank you. Additional question, Mrs Małgorzata Janowska.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u44	I've presented the stage of preparation for the first power auction. All these issues that precede the first power auction are kept on our side.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u48	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The issue of imports of hard coal into Poland from the territory of the Russian Federation is known to the Ministry of Energy and regularly monitored. Imports of anthracite should be seen primarily as a problem for the European Commission. Incomprehensible is the unequal treatment of occupied provinces of Crimea and Donetsk. Crimean goods are subject to an embargo, while goods - not only coal but all goods - originating in Donetsk were not restricted to their entry into the customs territory of the European Union. We were surprised to note that the territory of Donbas was not subject to customs sanctions, as it was done in the case of Crimea. It is the European Union that has legal tools to limit imports of coal from outside the European Union. A Member State of the European Union may not impose an import ban on its own regardless of what kind of goods it is. The self-introduction of import restrictions would be incompatible with the Community market. The recent increase in imports into Poland shows the demand for this raw material on the domestic market, but also demonstrates the attractiveness of the Polish market, which so far is used by exporters mainly from the Russian Federation. Polish mining is currently facing various kinds of challenges, which are consistently implemented. In accordance with the Union tariff for anthracite transport, a zero duty rate applies as for other types of hard coal. This means, regardless of the origin of the imported coal and the country from which it is shipped, that it is not subject to customs duties when it is transported to the European Union. In view of the general absence of a requirement to provide certificates of origin for goods exported from Russia, the European Union has not concluded or currently applies unilateral tariff preferences to preferential trade with Russia. No specific control measures are required for products imported from that country. Information about the transport of coal originating in eastern Ukraine as Russian coal can be considered as a signal to take control action, but it will be possible only if the proofs of origin of the goods were submitted to the customs authorities on their importation into Poland. Poland concluded a bilateral customs cooperation agreement before accession to the European Union. The Union has also concluded an agreement with Russia on mutual assistance in customs matters. On the basis of these agreements, verification activities may be carried out in order to verify the authenticity of the documents and the accuracy of the information contained therein. However, any findings that imported coal originated in Ukraine and not Russia will not result in another dimension of customs duties and imports of coal originating in Ukraine are not subject to any prohibitions as opposed to the carriage of goods originating in Crimea. The main supplier of hard coal to Poland is mainly the Russian Federation. The Czech Republic was the next for many years. Another major importers are Australia - in 2017 its share of imports amounted to 13%. , then Colombia - from 1% to 8%, followed by the USA, whose share in imports was 6.3%. Currently, it is the second largest importer with a share of 9,8% in general import. The introduction of coal into Poland takes place on two roads - land and sea. The close proximity of the main coal exporters on the one hand and the modernisation and increase of port capacity, as well as the conversion of export infrastructure to imports on the other, make the share of the land road and the sea road at the present time, January-May, even. Similar proportions are presented in the data held for 2017. In the series... on the Polish market there is coal derived from imports. One might be tempted to say that to some extent this is a natural phenomenon, for example due to a geographical pension. It must be noted whether the transport of coal has a significant share and impact on the final prices of this raw material, since transport costs can significantly increase the price of coal in the country. It is therefore much more cost-effective for consumers in the north of the country to purchase coal delivered to seaports or border crossings with Russia and Belarus. In order to show the broad context of imports of hard coal into Poland, we need to make a balance of imports and exports. In 2017, imports of coal from the Russian Federation amounted to 8.7 million tons with total imports of 13.3 million tons. Exports of Polish coal to the European Union and exports outside the European Union during the same period amounted to 6 million tons. Balance: imports and exports in total are 7.3 million tons, and after deduction of total imports from the Russian Federation we receive 2.7 million tons. The extraction of hard coal in Poland in 2017 amounted to 65.5 million t, which means that 7.3 million t as a total balance sheet represents 11% market share, while 2.7 million [[♪ The bell ♪]] That's only 4%. market share. The rise in global coal prices has another important social aspect: the entry of the energy sector into the mining sector has proved to be a very good decision. Energy companies, buying coal from Polish mines, gain financially because Polish coal is cheaper than coal on world markets.

The Voivode, Madame, only appoints a committee to assess the losses. And that's the difference. The committee shall consist of a representative of local government, agricultural government and an agricultural advisory centre. This is a basic instrument that is used to estimate losses. With regard to the guidelines that you call for for pastures, you are absolutely right here that pastures have also been affected by drought. However, these guidelines clearly state that in the case of estimates of damage to meadows, account should be taken of the period of occurrence and the number of swaths and the deadline for carrying them out, estimating the maximum losses. Their level is set: for two-sighted meadows - the first slice is 60%, the second slice is 40% , for triangular meadows - the first slice is 50% The second is 30%, the third is 20%. In assessing the losses in pastures, the first rotation, May-June - 50% should be taken into account. , second rotation, July-August - 30%, third rotation - 20%. In these guidelines, which are sent by the Minister responsible for agriculture and rural development, everything is described in detail how and what is to be estimated. And the pastures are included there. So here is the role for the committee to estimate the loss, not the voivodeship. The voivodeship's task is, first, to set up such a committee, second, when the local government has already asked for such a committee and has drawn up such a protocol, the voivodship shall approve losses of at least 30%. For the first time in history, something like this happened, that there are 1.5 billion zlotys dedicated to helping farmers affected by drought. In 2015, when there was approximately a similar drought, it was less than 450 million, so we have no scale of comparison. This is three times more aid for farmers targeted precisely at those affected by droughts. As far as the next part of this question is concerned, a greening derogation was applied in 2018. Fallow land may be considered as separate crops for the diversification of crops, even if grazing of animals has been carried out on such land or was intended for production purposes. In the context of interplons considered to be the ecological area of the EFA, farmers will be able to sow the interplon. Farmers who for the purpose of applying the practice EFA sowed an interspersal pad, but due to drought they could not perform this sowing at optimal agrotechnical time, they would be able to shorten the maintenance period of the interspersal pad for up to 6 weeks, provided that the ozime plants are sown on these land. I would like to say a few words about intangible heritage, in more detail, so that all Members are aware of how much has been done and, in general, the policy direction pursued by the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage. First of all, Poland ratified the 2003 UNESCO Convention on the protection of intangible cultural heritage, becoming a party to the 2003 UNESCO Convention on 16 August 2011. The ratification of the 2003 UNESCO Convention was enthusiastically accepted by the environment and thus met the expectations of both depositaries and NGOs themselves, cultural institutions, researchers, representatives of local government and state authorities. The implementation of the tasks arising from the provisions of the 2003 UNESCO Convention has been entrusted by the Minister of Culture and National Heritage to the National Heritage Institute, which you have just mentioned. In the structures of the National Heritage Institute there are 16 field branches throughout Poland. Representatives of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage and the National Heritage Institute are organizers of meetings in individual regions, during which they bring together the UNESCO 2003 Convention and the main issues of protection of intangible cultural heritage. What is the protection of intangible heritage? Following a public consultation, the Minister for Culture and National Heritage appointed a Council for the protection of intangible heritage, which brings together experts, namely representatives of the scientific community, NGOs and cultural institutions. Since 2013, the Minister for Culture and National Heritage has kept a national list of intangible cultural heritage, which was also mentioned by the Member. There are now 29 elements on it. The first entries in the national register of good practices in the protection of intangible cultural heritage were made, and the national list of intangible heritage is informative and is a form of dissemination of elements of intangible heritage in Poland. Groups, communities, NGOs, local authorities and individuals play a key role in the creation of a national list and a national register of good practices. Among the traditions declared included the traditions of dudzarski, lacemaking, embroidery, toying, falconing and braiding. At the initiative of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage, a programme was set up for the Master of Tradition, which is scholarly in nature, for masters who pass on their skills to subsequent generations in local environments. Intergenerational communication plays an important role in preserving traditional techniques, skills, access to traditional knowledge. The programme envisages a long-term perspective of systematic support for the master and the student, mutual relations, relationships and transmission of knowledge. Depositaries and researchers of tradition can also obtain state support in the program of folk and traditional culture. This programme includes financial support for the creators and institutions around the care of certain individuals. It is about exhibitions, the organisation of workshops for a wide range of continuers of these local traditions. A separate program is run by the Institute of Music and Dance, where the organizer is the Minister of Culture and National Heritage. These include the tradition master's school dedicated to the dissemination of traditional skills in instruments and traditional singing, and the master's school in the construction of folk instruments. A new programme of the Minister of Culture and National Heritage was also announced in 2018. It's a program dedicated to cities under 50,000. residents. The aim of the programme is to appreciate and disseminate knowledge about the traditional values present in the material and intangible culture of communities remote from large centres of cultural life. It is addressed to amateur folklore and theatre groups of reconstructive groups, organizations of local community members, e.g. groups of rural housewives, voluntary firefighters. This programme is interdisciplinary. It provides funding for both cultural events and publications, recordings, films, conferences and educational activities. Non-governmental organisations and local cultural institutions are entitled to submit applications in the programme. The funding may cover up to 95% the budget. Currently Poland expects the decision of the Intergovernmental Committee on intangible heritage. Let me just add that after 4 years in the UNESCO World Heritage Committee... The Ministry of National Education, introducing a reform, has just taken care of the education of children with special educational needs, introducing changes that a student with disabilities integrates into peer groups, which ensures that the needs of each student can be met. Such actions reduce the effects of disability and include children in the school community, which is so important and important for the development of each child. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u84	Thank you very much. The Secretary of State at the Ministry of National Education, Minister Marzena Machalek, is responsible.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u85	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. This is an important question and the answer to this question should start by defining what inclusive education is and how we see a child in inclusive education. Generally speaking, education that includes this is such a perception of a child, a student at school, in kindergarten, that each one of them is a student with special educational needs. The one who functions with disabilities is noticed, and the one who is very capable, and the one who is sometimes invisible in the classroom, because at first glance does not cause any problems. Education that includes seeing each student and providing him with the fullest possible developmental and educational opportunities, striving for the student to learn, raise, develop in the group, and, if necessary, to prepare individual educational activities for him. The main objective of inclusive education is to ensure that every student has a high quality of education and conditions to fully realise the development potential at school, in kindergarten, as close as possible to the place of confusion. It's not about us taking the kids out, looking for places, it's about being close to being in their environment. These needs, this diversity of pupils' development and educational needs, e.g. disability, health, life situation or special abilities are understood as potential, as a resource - not as a problem. Because every human being is unique, and the task of school is to find and develop the strengths and abilities of every child and to provide conditions for harmonious development. If a child encounters problems, difficulties in development, e.g. In acquiring knowledge and skills, whether in operation, being with peers, kindergarten or school should take all measures to remove those barriers that lie on the side of the school. These are architectural barriers, organizational barriers or in terms of methods and actions towards the student. It's a school job. But support in assessing the needs of students - because this must also be assessed, because in order to be able to act and take these actions, you need to see the needs - to identify the causes of the student's difficulties, to find effective methods of supporting activity, participation of the student in life and kindergarten, it requires the work of psychologists and pedagogical counselors, but also properly prepared teachers and educators. The Ministry of Education, so seeing education inclusive and thus pursuing inclusive education, is working intensively to prepare legal solutions that will support high-quality education - inclusive education. The work that we have already done and in which we are in the process was preceded by a really broad consultation with associations, with parents whose children operate with disabilities. We talk, listen, and, on the basis of this, prepare legal solutions. There's been a task force. The special educational needs that Minister Anna Zalewska has set up, and within this team, of course, the staff of the Ministry, but also high-quality specialists and representatives of environments that are very much interested in inclusive education solutions. These solutions, which we are preparing, result from solutions of the international classification of the functioning of disability and health. The activities carried out in the framework of the preparation of the model of education for pupils with special educational needs based precisely on recommendations and conclusions from reports of various Polish and national actors have led to the development of a project. This project was dealt with by a team of experts set up in 2017. But already in 2016, a project on the development of standards for diagnosis of developmental and educational needs and diagnostic tools began. In 2017, changes were made to the legislation that introduced this model into current legal solutions. These changes, first of all, provide more opportunities to adapt the organisation and education to the needs of pupils who face school difficulties, secondly, expand the group of students who may be covered by individual activities - not narrower, our solutions broaden these opportunities - thirdly, point to a different approach to disability issues, in line with the UN Convention and the Convention on the Rights of Persons with Disabilities. It has also been pointed out that barriers to the functioning can also result from the school's failure to adapt to the needs of pupils and not just from restrictions on the health or disability of pupils. They encourage schools to take action to limit the exclusion of pupils with disabilities from the peer group. I think it is worth stressing that these new solutions really require more commitment from the school, teachers, are more difficult to organize, but they are certainly conducive to the fuller development of our students. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u147	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Government of the Republic of Poland a bill amending the Act on the Office of the Minister of National Defence and the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. This project is commonly called a small reform of the SKiD, a small reform of the management and command system in the Polish Army. Why is it small? Because this is the first stage of our actions related to ordering the situation, in terms of command in the Polish Army. The next phase will be a project that will restore the command of types of Armed Forces, which will eventually put the situation in order. Why have we decided on the stages of the change in the management and command system? To follow this process, to keep this process under control and to have a satisfactory effect as soon as possible, because a major reform of the management and command system will certainly take much longer, and we are living in a situation where we need to be aware of the dangers that lie ahead of our homeland. Therefore, we need to react quickly and efficiently. Ladies and gentlemen, I'll start by referring to the Latin maxim: si vis pacem, para bellum - you want peace, get ready for war. This is undoubtedly the maximum of the tasks and objectives that stand before me as the Minister of National Defence. We want peace, but in order to gain peace, the Polish Army must be, first, more numerous, second, equipped with modern equipment and, third, must be more firmly embedded in the structures of the North Atlantic Alliance. And these three projects, these three objectives we're pursuing. Firstly, the Polish Army must be more numerous, therefore we organize the process of increasing Polish Army units. This process concerns among others. the establishment of a new tactical union of the Polish Army, i.e. a new division. The 18th division of the Polish Army was established, it is located east of the Vistula, the command of this division is located in Siedlce. I've also called a division commander, this is an experienced commander, this is an officer who's been in charge for the last 15 years. The Giżycka Mechanized Brigade, i.e. the troops working daily within the framework of the eFP with NATO allies, and especially with the United States Army. This is a process - increasing the number of the Polish Army. Secondly, the Polish Army must be equipped with modern equipment, therefore I signed in March this year an agreement with our American partner on the equipment of the Polish Army in Patriot rockets. This is indeed the state-of-the-art equipment, this is an in-depth change in terms of defence capabilities of the Polish Army. Negotiations on the implementation of the next programmes are ongoing, so this is a process in which we gradually exchange equipment from post-Soviet to state-of-the-art equipment, equipment compatible with the equipment used by allies, equipment which guarantees the defence capabilities of our country. And the third element - the stronger settlement of Poland within the North Atlantic Alliance. This is served by decisions made, decisions carried out in connection with the NATO Summit of July 2016, i.e. the presence in Poland of allied forces, U.S. troops, British troops, Romanian and Croatian troops, but also the activity of the Polish Army. As you know, the Polish Army is stationed in Latvia, the Polish Army is stationed in Romania. We are also active with regard to the quota in Afghanistan, the quota in Iraq, the quota in Kosovo - these are the quotas of the Nativ troops, but also within the framework of the European Union's activities - the Polish Army is in Bosnia and Herzegovina, the Polish Army is in the Mediterranean, this is the last EU mission that we are also participating in, so we are active. In this way we build our country's position within the North Atlantic Alliance and within the European Union, in this way we show our solidarity, but in this way the Polish Army also acquires skills, trains with allies, learns exactly the procedures associated with the daily operation of allies' troops, so this process is undoubtedly very valuable for defence capabilities of the Polish Army. Ladies and gentlemen, and what is the subject of the project, the change of command system. Here we are repairing the error that was made by introducing the current command system on 1 January 2014, a non-transparent system, a system that arouses, well, such unpleasant interest of our allies, because our allies ask who to talk to when it comes to exercises. military - with one command, because one command organizes specific exercises, or with another command, because another command organizes specific exercises. It doesn't make sense, ladies and gentlemen, it doesn't make sense. In the military, in every army, there should be a chain of command, there should be clear rules on crisis response. Therefore, the submission, which I have the honour of proposing to you, sits as the first soldier of the Republic of Poland head of the General Staff. He will be the one under the command of both the General Commander of the Armed Forces and the Operational Commander of the Armed Forces. As part of this change, we also assign the Support Inspector under the supervision of the Chief of Staff. These are the actions that result from the assessment of the situation that result from the observations that result from drawing conclusions on the situation that is taking place today. I would like to stress that it is important that the Polish Army is ready to resist aggression if it were to happen. And since we are living in troubled times, I will appeal to words, familiar words, but I think they are worth recalling today as well. Sleep. President Lech Kaczyński in Tbilisi in 2008 after the Russian attack on Georgia said: Today Georgia, tomorrow Ukraine, then the Baltic States and then Poland. The geopolitical situation changed in 2016, because then the decisions made at the NATO Summit introduced a change in stationing - I was just talking about it - allied forces in Poland and Polish troops also in allied countries. But let me remind you that in 2008 the rulers of that time underestimated these words, said that Russia is a responsible partner, that one can rely on what the Russian leaders preach and, above all, one Russian leader, who is still in power, was also in power at that time. They didn't even verify their grades after the Smolensk tragedy, they kept saying the same thing. The year came 2014, attacking Russia on Ukraine, the occupation of Crimea. Fortunately, in Poland the power changed in 2015. The government of Law and Justice, the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, sets itself the objective of ensuring the safety of Poland. Hence, as I said, the implementation of these three points and hence the change in the introduction of a transparent command system in the Polish Army. I'll end by recalling Napoleon's words. Well, Napoleon said that in the war, he wins who makes the least mistakes. We are correcting the mistake made in 2014. High Izbo! I am asking you to accept the Government's submission. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u148	Thank you, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wojciech Buczak, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u149	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position on the draft law amending the Act on the Office of the Minister of National Defence and the Act on the universal obligation to defend the Republic of Poland. The aim of the proposed solutions is to simplify the management and command system of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, as well as to create conditions and organizational structures operating in the time of peace which are as close to those of the war. They will ensure a uniform division of competences and tasks and a clear separation of command levels in the Polish Army. The proposed amendments to the laws will also adapt the existing Armed Forces Command System to the solutions adopted in NATO. In recent years, changes have taken place in the international environment, which have a direct impact on the security of the Republic of Poland. This has contributed, among other things. to significantly increase spending on our Armed Forces and upgrade equipment. Nevertheless, in order for the modern Armed Forces to function efficiently, it is necessary to construct the chain of command in a way that ensures a clear definition of the roles of the different levels and levels of command. Analysis of the existing Armed Forces control and command system leads to the conclusion that current command system solutions, e.g. The combination of strategic and operational levels in the national chain of command is not compatible with NATO and most of the Alliance countries. Within NATO, the principle is clear separation of these levels. The same solutions should be used in the Polish Armed Forces system. This will ensure efficient and effective cooperation with NATO command structure and allies. The last change in the management and command system, which entered into force in January 2014, basically remodeled the structure and tasks of the control and command bodies of the Polish Armed Forces. Experience, analysis and exercise so far show that there are many problems with the current system. The conclusions, which result from this, indicate the need to strengthen the strategic powers of the Head of the General Staff of the Polish Army, in accordance with the command model in force in NATO. According to the proposed solutions, the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army, being the highest active soldier and candidate for commander-in-chief of the Armed Forces, will have effective instruments to represent the Polish Armed Forces and will be able to agree on all issues concerning the consistency of operational planning in the national and ally dimension. At the same time, the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army, being the highest soldier in active military service, will lead the entire Armed Forces. Until the territorial defence forces have achieved their full capacity to act, the commander of the territorial defence forces will be directly subject to the Minister of National Defence, except that, after the completion of this process, WOTs, like the other types of Armed Forces, will be subordinated to the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army. Such a solution will allow for an undistorted process of forming this extremely important for the security of the state type of Armed Forces. However, this solution does not exempt the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army from carrying out planning work related to the use of this type of Armed Forces in the event of a crisis or war. In general, in order to ensure continuity of command under extraordinary circumstances, in which the state may find itself, such as war or martial law, until the command of the commander-in-chief of the Armed Forces is taken over, this function will be carried out by the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army against all commanders of types of Armed Forces. At the same time, the new wording of the article. 7a will determine that the head of the General Staff of the Polish Army is the highest soldier in the field of active military service. In general, the consequences of the proposed amendment are [[♪ The bell ♪]] Introducing a one-man responsibility for commanding the entire Armed Forces, a gradual reform of the management system...
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u150	Thank you very much, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u151	...the possibility of undisturbed action in the event of an external threat...
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u152	Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u153	...and adapt the command system of the Armed Forces to NATO command structures. On behalf of the parliamentary club, I request that work be carried out on this bill in committees. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u154	Thank you very much. Please, Mr Cezary Grabarczyk, Civic Platform.

I ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration of Minister Krzysztof Kozłowski to present the justification for the project.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u193	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a government bill amending the Personal Evidence Act and some other laws aimed at implementing an electronic identity card. The new evidence will be a secure and free electronic communication tool with the administration, health services and commercial operators. The new identity card will, as has been the case so far, confirm the identity and nationality of the holder and entitle him to cross the borders of certain countries. The use of new electronic functionalities of the new identity card will be voluntary and will be a stand-alone decision of each e-proof holder. Electronic proof will be used to authenticate the holder in public administration e-services and to sign electronic documents. It will also allow confirmation of the identity card holder's presence in external IT systems, including in particular health systems. E-proof will also allow the use of automatic gates at airports in the European Union. This document will also include a space to include a qualified electronic signature certificate in accordance with EU law. The promotion of the functionality of electronic identity cards should improve processes, especially in the state and local authorities, improve the service of the citizen while improving the security of these services. The popularization of e-services will in turn translate into a lower burden on the office and efficiency of proceding. Using the functionality of confirmation of presence, among others. in confirming the reception of medical services, it will in turn contribute to the sealing of the health care system, which will translate into savings on other benefits carried out in this area. It is planned that all personal evidence will be provided with a certificate of confirmation of presence, while the certificate for identification and authentication will be provided in personal evidence of persons with full legal capacity and persons with limited capacity to do so. At the same time, activation of the certificate of identification and authentication will not be mandatory. Uninterested persons will not be forced to use this functionality while maintaining the possibility to run it at any time, at their own will. The personal signature certificate shall be included in the personal evidence of those persons who have the full capacity to act and when submitting an application for an identity card they shall consent to the inclusion of that certificate. This will also apply to the personal evidence of minors who, during the period of validity of the identity card, end the 18th year of age if their representative consents, of course. The personal signature certificate will therefore be included in the personal ID of those who wish to use this functionality. The use of personal e-signatures by a public entity will have the same effect the signature itself and, in the case of another entity, the same effect shall exist, provided that that entity and the holder of the electronic identity card so agree. A personal signature will be an advanced electronic signature within the meaning of EU law. The draft law, which is submitted, under consideration by the High House, also provides for an institution to suspend, for a maximum of 14 days, certificates placed in a layer of electronic identity card in the event of a temporary loss of control of that document. The suspension of certificates will also result in the suspension of the validity of the identity card and the operations carried out during the suspension period or the cancellation of the certificates will not have legal effect. If within 14 days the holder of the identity card does not revoke the suspension of the certificates, the identity card shall be automatically revoked. At the same time, the proposed rules introduce the possibility of suspending and withdrawing certificates, as well as reporting the loss of identity cards automatically, without the involvement of an official, using a dedicated electronic service for this purpose. The launch of new evidence is planned for March 2019. There will be no need to exchange existing documents. I'd really like it to be accented and sounded. These existing documents shall remain valid until the time limits laid down therein have expired. Those interested in new functionalities will be given the opportunity to apply for a new e-proof free of charge. Poland is one of the few European countries in which no identity card with an electronic layer has yet been issued. The electronic evidence already exists in Austria, Belgium, Croatia, Portugal, Estonia, Finland, France, Spain, Lithuania, Germany, Sweden, Hungary and Italy. This document is most commonly used for identification and authentication and for electronic signature. The electronic functionality is used by adults, most often on a voluntary basis - this is what happens, among others. Spain and Luxembourg. Similar solutions were adopted in the proposed law. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The project presented is a significant step towards promoting safe electronic communication and allows Poland to keep the funds obtained from the European Union for this purpose. I ask the High House to adhere to the submitted project. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

It is intended to contribute to wider and faster access for citizens to public administration, health services, as well as widely understood commercial services. For the provider of such a service, it is often the lack of direct contact with the provider, which results from the doubts about the declared identity and age of the person to whom the service is to be provided. The Panaceum for these problems has become two tools: authentication mechanisms and an electronic signature. The document described in the bill is intended to unambiguously and undeniably confirm the identity of the citizen, to authenticate in e-services of public administration and to sign electronic documents, and to allow to confirm the presence at a specific place and time using ICT systems. A new identity card, as the government plans, will have an ICAO application, i.e. travel document with biometric features of a facial image. The new model of identity card will include data in the electronic layer, which, together with data known only to the holder of this proof, will allow both to authenticate it in the ICT systems where online services are provided and, above all, to provide electronic documents with an electronic signature. A signature called a personal signature will constitute an advanced electronic signature within the meaning of Regulation (EU) No 910/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council or of the eIDAS Regulation and can be submitted without the need for access to the Internet. The certificate of confirmation of the presence or receipt of medical services by electronic means will contribute to the sealing of the health care system, which will allow savings to be made, for example, for other benefits. The Polish party informed the European Commission at the end of 2015 about the planned continuation of the project of the ID system. The proposed law does not change the issue of personal evidence. It merely limits itself to regulating issues related to functionality in the layer of electronic identity card. The draft law provides for the regulation of the issue of exchange and cancellation of identity cards, including e.g. the loss of its validity. The government provides for a special mode when one of the certificates is blocked, e.g. when the holder of the identity card has lost the unlocking code or the personal electronic signature certificate is no longer valid. The source of financing of personal evidence with an electronic layer is the state budget. The evaluation of project results will be carried out by the Interministerial Steering Committee on e-Project with the assumption that at least 5 million electronic evidence will be released by the end of 2021. High Izbo! At this point, the comments and views expressed by the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection must not be ignored, and this is the issue of replacing the PESEL number with any other identifier, which would be a gigantic undertaking, resulting in the reconstruction of all functioning systems. The PESEL number shall be the identification number of the citizen referred to in the implementing rules of the eIDAS Regulation and shall comply with the GDPR. My club is not opposed to the adoption of an amendment to the law, but, given the wide application of its subject matter and the need for correlation with the rules already in force, we consider it necessary to subject the matter to a detailed analysis, so that - which is often the case - we do not adopt the Bubble Act with its negative consequences for the citizen as well as for other entities with which the citizen will meet on a daily basis. My club supports the motion to refer the project to further work on the committee. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

In our view, this is the amount that will fully meet the budgetary needs of local government units, the more that the research that we have carried out shows that it will not be more time-consuming to issue such evidence. than the issue of a traditional proof. This procedure will be de facto different because you will need to assign a PIN number using the encoding device. And this is the one and only element that on the citizen's side will be a certain addition to the existing legal situation. Mr Raniewicz also asked about security issues in the event of the loss of this document, i.e. physical loss of identity card. As I said in this first part of the speech, it will be possible to reserve it for a period of 14 days in the electronic system. On the other hand, any person who wishes to invalidate the evidence or to reserve its electronic layer will be able to do so via the Internet, the electronic system, using the PIN number, which in turn will be given when issuing the evidence, and will also be able to do so personally or by a proxy at the municipal office. Mr Raniewicz also asked about the application issues. Thank you so much for that question. Indeed, this digitisation of the functioning of social, economic life requires that we move more and more services to the electronic sphere, so that the administration - and the government, and the local government - is increasingly open and realized for the benefit of citizens, in favour of the so-called. end-users, those services in a digitised form. We are working together with the Ministry of Digitalization to create maintenance applications that citizens will be able to use. But in order for us to do this, first this law, which is submitted to the High House for consideration, has to be adopted, is the basis for further action. Mr Marek Wójcik asked about the safety of using proof. As I said, it is possible to reserve it for the first 14 days, and later - total deactivation in different channels and personally, and by proxy, and through the electronic system. I am pleased that the High Chamber, the Members of the House, has been open to this main request, the foundation of the bill, namely free of charge, and the exchangeability of personal evidence. Evidence will be made available free of charge to all citizens, including when a person decides to exchange his current identity card for this already modern e-proof. Mr Wójcik also pointed out the need to include in this layer and material and electronic proof of personal information about the citizen's blood group. This is, of course, an idea worthy of consideration, but we must bear in mind the fact that a system would first have to be created that would collect such data. A separate legislative decision would be needed in this respect, which would allow the Minister of Health to take and store such information. It would be a little difficult, however, to appreciate the attention of the Member, to enter the blood type on the basis of the citizen's statement. You can forget, you can not remember, and in a situation of health or life threat such information means a great deal, misusing could cause serious difficulties. Mr Bartosz Józwiak and Mr Jerzy Meysztowicz spoke very strongly about the right to privacy. The High Sejm, it is clear that our ambition, all of us, and the government side, and the parliamentary side, is to create a good law that will not be disproportionately or inappropriately interfering with the right to citizens' privacy. And this bill was thus created. Any possible sharing of information from the PESEL register, from the Register of Personal Evidence, will take place under the existing rules, i.e. under the control of the relevant supervisory authorities, as well as by the court, depending on the categories of data made available and the purpose of making them available. I would like to make it very clear that this project, which has been addressed to the High House, does not result in a qualitative change as regards the access of individual eligible entities, including courts, prosecutors, special services, to information that will be included in the system containing personal evidence data. Mr Meysztowicz also pointed to the functionality that we wanted very much. Namely, on the basis of this new e-proof, our citizens, Poles, will be able to continue to travel within the Schengen area, using the identity card itself, and this novelty and this great functionality is the possibility of using ABC gates, i.e. gates that enable automatic identification of a person without having to stand in queues. More and more countries decide to do so and use this functionality. We would also like Poles not to have to stand in line at other airports or border crossings, but to use ABC gates. Therefore, this evidence will contain data that are subject to ICAO standard. Mr Zbigniew Sosnowski pointed out this remark, which sounds here and is notified by the President of the Office for Personal Data Protection. This is not a new remark. It concerns the use of the PESEL number and the significant dissemination of this number. At the moment, it appears that such a basic identification number should remain the PESEL number. If we decided to identify citizens through other identifiers, other markings, this would be equivalent to the need to build new systems that are very expensive. I don't think that would be entirely appropriate at this stage at this point. Mr Paweł Pudłowski asked about the guarantee of implementation within the deadline of this EU commitment. I would like to assure you, Mr President and the High House, that this deadline, which is set out in the bill, will be respected if the High House wishes to bend over this project and, if possible, proceed immediately, and will not have to reimburse the European Commission. We on the government side, i.e. a certain administrative structure, an IT structure, are prepared for this. Here I wanted to appreciate very good cooperation with the Ministry of Digitalization, which the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration in this work actively supports. Mr Piotr Pyzik asked about a certain internationalisation of the identity card and a certain durability of this project, that is, a philosophy based on the use of e-proof. The data we have shows that for at least 20 years it will be an IT tool that citizens of other European Union countries will be able to use freely. And from the point of view of the technological process, its progress in computerization is a tool that for 20 years should allow citizens to communicate conveniently, comfortably with government, local government, banks, institutions of economic life. Ms Agnieszka Kołacz-Leszczyńska also expressed her concern about the timely implementation of the EU commitment. I would like to assure you once again that this will be achieved. I encourage you to cooperate fairly and ask for it. I am convinced that together we will be able to carry out this project immediately. Mr Arkadiusz Marchewka asked a question about continuing to work on this e-document, mapping the material evidence in the telephone application. This is a question that should de facto sound to the Minister of Digitization, who hosts the work. It's in this area. My knowledge is that the Minister of Digitization will continue to work on this application or this e-document. It also welcomes the fact that citizens will be able to choose between material evidence, what each of us has in plastic form in its pocket, evidence with electronic layer, whose project is submitted to the High House as well as an e-document that will not have such functionalities as e-proof. However, we want to leave citizens with full freedom, freedom to choose which means of identification they want to use. Mr Grzegorz Wojciechowski asked about the possibility of using e-proof abroad. We need to distinguish two points. Firstly, e-proof will continue to serve as a document confirming identity as well as citizenship. And this is the document on which our citizens will be able to travel inside the Schengen area without having to have a passport. In the course of the discussion, there was also an argument that we should eliminate personal evidence and, in return, allow citizens to have a passport. This is an idea, only that we have to take into account one fact: issuing an identity card is free of charge and the Treasury can afford it, while issuing a passport is a payment, so the transition to passports would be an additional burden for the state budget, because then they would have to be made free and widely accessible to citizens. I will return to Mr Grzegorz Wojciechowski's question. Proof with an electronic layer shall, in principle, be the document certifying and the identity and nationality of the person concerned. In the context of the regulation contained in the Schengen Code, this is a sufficient travel document which allows for crossing borders within the Schengen area. On the other hand, with regard to access to administrative functionality via an electronic layer of identity card, each country has its own databases and does not provide for the possibility of using a foreign identity card. In other words, the Polish citizen will be able to authenticate his identity with his signature, possibly with his electronic signature. These are services that are provided by the Polish government, local government or institutions of economic life, such as: banks and companies. On the other hand, there is one extremely important feature that the Member has pointed out. This is an ICAO standard, which is included in the new identity card, in e-Professor. Indeed, biometric data will be encrypted there, but they are encrypted according to ICAO International Standard and thus the person will be able to cross borders using ABC gateways, so this functionality is actually preserved. If you wish to discuss it, of course we are at your disposal, if the Sejm so decides in the course of further work on the bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

This was translated into the conclusions of the General Affairs Council, which were adopted at the end of June, at the end of the Bulgarian Presidency. These conclusions, in our view, could have been much more ambitious, could have been more forward-looking, but the fact that they were adopted after a very negative experience more than a year ago is a success. This is very important in political terms. Another thing I would like to say here is security and stability. This is an extremely pressing, politically very crucial dimension of the Union's policy in recent months and years. On the one hand, we have been faced with a persistent patch, a deadlock in the work on the reform of the European asylum system at technical level and at the level of the Justice Council. At the same time, the determination of the Polish government and the resistance of the Polish government to inappropriate and inconclusive solutions based on transnational migration management and refugee movement at the end of the Bulgarian Presidency led to a real breakthrough. The European Council not only revised its temporary expectations regarding the completion of the reform of the asylum system, but also set out very clear guidelines for home affairs ministers on how this new compromise is to be shaped, it is a new opening in the field of asylum policy of the European Union, a policy which has broken a great deal of blood in the EU, including in political terms. Firstly, EU policy in this respect should focus on the distinction between economic migration and the refugee movement. These are completely different legal conditions from the point of view of international law. Humanitarian responsibility towards these two groups is also completely different. Without a clear distinction between one group and the other, EU policies in this area cannot be imagined. It is a policy which would be subject to consensus, which is necessary to reduce the political tensions that are still taking place in the Union in relation to this matter. The second thing is to stop migration. It is clear that the Union's policy on this issue is aimed at halting migration. It was a Polish postulate from the very beginning. From the outset, we have pointed out that Europe - no matter what we think - is unable to accommodate such a large group of people who, for very different reasons, often dramatic, are now outside their home in North Africa and the Middle East. In this context, the objective of EU policy, where the Union can play a positive role, is above all to stop and restore control of migration flows at our borders, in particular southern but also eastern borders. From the point of view of Poland, there is no less important aspect - today it is not dramatic, but one day it can become such - there is also full control and shared responsibility for the durability of the eastern border. This cannot be done without effective cooperation with third countries. That's the design. This perspective has been outlined, suggesting the establishment of land-based platforms for those crossing the sea border, which is the most difficult to control effectively. These platforms are to be set up in consultation with third countries as the most appropriate solution for this. And the last thing I think is the most important thing. The European Council made it clear that all internal solidarity mechanisms must assume the voluntary admission of refugees to the borders of the Member States of the European Union. That's the outline. I am not saying that this is a complete process, but this is an outline of a compromise that allows us to think more calmly about what can happen in the field of migration and asylum policy in the European Union. If we lead to this compromise, I hope that this whole House will be proud of the determination and stubbornness of Polish offices, the Polish government in this matter. They had to endure very much political pressure for years, going from very different directions, also from within, related to ideas that were simply wrong and inefficient. The vast majority of countries active in the field of defence policy of the European Union are also a member of NATO. The Minister presented the course and achievements of the Bulgarian Presidency through its priorities and key issues from Poland's point of view. The Bulgarian Presidency focused on the following issues: firstly, the future of Europe and young people - economic growth and social cohesion, secondly, the European perspective and communication with the Western Balkans, thirdly, security and stability in a strong and united Europe, fourthly, the digital economy and the skills of the future. As regards the implementation of the priority: the future of Europe and young people, the Bulgarian Presidency focused on the preparation of the MFF negotiations after 2020. The Presidency stressed the particular role of cohesion policy and the common agricultural policy, which was in line with Poland's position. I would like to point out here that Poland has a critical attitude towards many elements of the European Commission's proposal on the next multiannual financial framework. Fully recognising the new priorities, it cannot be accepted that they are financed to a large extent at the expense of Treaty policies, particularly cohesion policy and the common agricultural policy. Poland, however, is ready to overcome the negotiating difficulties as soon as possible. The Presidency also continued its efforts to reform the Economic and Monetary Union, focusing on work on completing the Banking Union and strengthening the European Stability Mechanism. The second priority of the Presidency is the European perspective and communication with the Western Balkans. In May of this year, the European Union-Western Balkans summit took place in Sofia. This is the first summit on this level in many years. In particular, it was devoted to various dimensions of communication between the Union and the Balkans. Poland welcomed the results of the summit and stressed the importance of the Via Carpatia transport corridor, infrastructure and digital connections, ensuring diversification of energy sources and the role of the Tri-Morch initiative in filling infrastructure gaps. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Another challenge faced by the Bulgarian Presidency was security and stability issues in a strong, united Europe. The priority was to implement mechanisms to increase the security of Union citizens, improve border protection and effective migration management. The Presidency sought consensus on the reform of the Common European Asylum System, but Poland did not support the way in which the Dublin Regulation was being worked and maintained its opposition to mandatory and automatic relocation. The Commission for European Union Affairs, considering this subject to be very important, considered at its meeting information on new European Union defence initiatives: in the framework of permanent structured cooperation, a coordinated annual defence review and the European Defence Fund. With regard to the digital economy and the skills of the future, the Presidency has sought to remove barriers to trade within the Union and has acted not only in the area of the Digital Single Market. The Presidency has concluded negotiations on the Posting of Workers Directive in the framework of the provision of services, but Poland is negatively assessing the final text of the Directive. The compromise we have worked out is unsatisfactory for us. As far as the mobility package is concerned, the Council of the European Union has ultimately failed to reach an agreement in the social and market part, so work will continue through the Austrian Presidency. The statutory deduction in force today amounts to 0.2% of the amount of contributions paid by the Social Security Office, which is the institution collecting these contributions to the National Health Fund. The assumption is that in 2019 this deduction will be reduced to 0.175% which will leave the National Health Fund with an increase of 18 900 000. zł. This is what the National Health Fund is going to use to carry out its new task. The Act also provides for the extension of the tasks carried out by the National Health Fund in this respect to IT and computerisation tasks for the provision of health services. However, I would like to say that these are not all the costs involved in implementing the idea of appointing a medical assistant to life. Plus, there's the cost of training medical assistants. The impact assessment attached to the draft law assumes that there will be a need to train about 300,000. persons. These are only assumptions, because we do not know what group of doctors will decide to authorize medical assistants to issue medical certificates, and what will be the number of doctors who will want to issue these certificates themselves. So these are very cautious estimates. In fact, these estimates are based on the assumption that all doctors or all persons authorised to issue certificates of incapacity for work will decide to appoint such a medical assistant. We have about 150,000 such doctors in Poland. As I said, the assumption is that doctors will decide to authorize up to two people to issue such a medical certificate. The costs associated with training will be borne by the Social Insurance Institution and will be approx. PLN 180 million, and I also pay attention to the high caution in estimating these costs. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u289	Thank you, Member. Please, Mrs Bernadette Krynick, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u290	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position on the government draft law amending the law on how to determine the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medicinal entities, print No. 2793. The most important change in this draft law is the extension of the scope of the subjective law of 8 June 2017 on how to determine the lowest basic salary of medical professionals employed in medical establishments for basic activities, other than those performing medical professions, which, within the meaning of the amendment, are persons employed as part of the employment relationship in a medical entity that does not perform a medical profession, which, however, carry out work remaining in connection with the provision of health services in the organizational cells of a medical establishment. According to the mechanism set out in the Act, the target amount of the lowest basic salary for the added group of basic activities with a labour factor of 0,53 cannot be less than PLN 2664 gross. This ceiling will be reached in 2020. At the same time, it should be stressed that the amended law regulates the level of the lowest basic salary, so it is more beneficial for workers than for the universal minimum wage law. Staff members of medicinal products shall be entitled to statutory salary allowances, which shall be calculated on the basis of the basic salary of the workers. Therefore, the increase in basic salary results in an automatic increase in wage-related income. Consequently, the full remuneration of a staff member employed in a medicinal entity, even if determined by the employer at the level governed by the law of the lowest remuneration, will normally not be limited to the amount of that lowest basic salary and may be much higher than it is. In addition, the project amends professional groups with numbers 7 to 9 in the Annex to the Act of 8 June 2017 on the transfer of nurses and midwives employed in positions requiring a Master's degree in nursing and midwifery, but not requiring specialization, from the existing group 9 to group 8. It is still very important that the draft law requires the implementation of the amendments introduced by the Act of 8 June 2017 in respect of increases in the basic salaries of basic workers other than medical professionals and nurses and midwives whose working rate will be increased with effect from 1 July 2018. Another important change is the introduction of a deadline by 15 June each year for the issue by the Head of the medicinal product or by the entity making up the salary increase arrangements. At present, the Act sets out the date on which the employer is required to increase the basic salaries of workers covered by the regulation. That period shall be 1 July each year. Before that date, an employer who does not agree with the trade union organisations or employees' representatives by 31 May will have to issue an appropriate order. However, there is currently no time limit in the Law for the adoption of that Order, so the introduction of the deadline for issuing the Order will improve the annual implementation of the statutory obligation to raise basic salaries. Finally, I would like to stress that this law ensures a systematic annual increase in salaries in the area of healthcare workers. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I ask you to continue to process the bill and to state that the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the draft law presented by the government. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The record of the conversation is: God, this deletion is ill-written, on the threshold of rejection. Such discussions and such attempts to corrupt the Supreme Court were possible due to the fact that the Supreme Court is currently examining cassation proceedings with total freedom, single-personity and practically without detailed justification. High Seym! The basic principle of anti-corruption is that many eyes should never allow one person to decide that, as far as possible, others should look at his hands. The one-man power of a judge is the great power, the power to decide the fate of people, their property, their lives, and it is very dangerous. The initiative of the Committee on Petitions aims to change this. We want a proper, decent procedure for handling cassation complaints, so that they are dealt with in full seriousness. The argument that such a change will fall into the Supreme Court's avalanche of cases is frivolous. The impact of cassation complaints to the Supreme Court, several thousand complaints per year, is comparable with the impact of cases on the average size of the district court, and the Supreme Court does not conduct evidence proceedings, does not question witnesses. So let's not get ahead of ourselves. That the Supreme Court will be pinned down with an avalanche of cases that will affect him. It certainly won't stay, and even if it stays, let's ask ourselves whether this is about the comfort of the judges' work or justice. After all, if it turns out that the Supreme Court is too small to accept all cassation complaints, then the Supreme Court will have to be enlarged, because justice is not for the court, it is for the justice.
