ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u312	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the draft law tabled by the Committee on Petitions amending the Civil Procedure Code Act, Parliamentary Printing No 2697. This proposal concerns changes in the rules governing the handling of cassation complaints by the Supreme Court. The aim of the amendment is to eliminate the so-called "deficiencies." the institution of the pre-court, i.e. a preliminary review of a single-member cassation complaint at a private meeting, where very generally formulated criteria decide on the acceptance of a single-member cassation complaint. It's an art. 398 c.p.c. These basic conditions are an important legal issue in the case, and it is also an interpretation of rules which raise doubts, or the existence of discrepancies in judgments, that the proceedings are null and void and that the cassation action is not justified. The project therefore calls for the identification of three-person complaints without pre-court. The proposal also eliminates reform ruling by the Supreme Court, repealing the Article. 398 Code of Civil Procedure, which was not properly justified in this project. We have the opinion of stakeholders for this project, among others. The Supreme Court's opinion, which points out that, in the absence of a pre-trial ruling in 2000, more than 8 000 were due in the Civil Chamber. cases to be dealt with, several years of proceedings in respect of a cassation complaint have taken place. When a pre-trial is introduced, I will remind you that these pre-trial orders were not immediately justified, only since 2007, exactly from 16 June, after the entry into force of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. The so-called complaint could also be considered. horizontal, i.e. in the composition of three judges, as is apparent from the opinion of the Supreme Court. This is a complaint against the order to refuse an action or to rule on a pre-trial hearing of three judges rather than one. The Opinion of the Supreme Bar Council - in favour of the abolition of the pre-trial procedure, the Opinion of the National Judicial Council - against the abolition of the pre-trial procedure, as this would paralyze the actions of the Civil Chamber, the Public Prosecutor General - against the abolition of the pre-trial procedure, but it also takes into consideration the identification of three-members and calls for a corps of specialised agents who would only have the right to lodge a cassation complaint. There is also the government's position on this matter. The government is in favour of leaving the court. He points out in his opinion that it should contain a full justification, with which, you know, there are problems, because sometimes these justifications are very laconic. Of course, it also claims that there will be a significant increase in the number of cases affecting the Supreme Court in the absence of such a pre-court. There is also the question of excluding from the competence of the Supreme Court the powers of substantive examination, i.e. to deliver a reformation verdict where this project is taking it. A wider debate is certainly needed to answer these questions. Another change proposed by the designers is the exclusion of the possibility for the Supreme Court to give a judgment on the substance of the case, as well as the repeal of the judgments and the rejection or termination of the proceedings. These proposals have no reasonable justification. First of all, they can lead to an extension of the procedure. The Supreme Court rarely gives reform judgments. This is the case where there are no circumstances which could support the annulment of the judgment and the referral of the case to be re-examined and at the same time allows the dispute to be brought to an end, which reduces the duration of the proceedings. The changes made will therefore lead to practical difficulties and will not bring any benefits. The proposed changes are highly controversial and, as I stressed earlier, require deeper reflection. Given that the proposed amendments are the reaction of the committee to the petition addressed to it, we are in favour of referring the draft to the committee in order to make a fair assessment of them. The justice system is an extremely complicated system and such a single catching of elements without taking a holistic view, without considering where the causes of the wrong functioning of the courts are, leads us astray. And the reasons are that it's common courts that do not work. The common courts are a mess. And why do they work like that? You have to answer that question. And here in this House, there has never been such a debate. What are the reasons why judges in Poland give such bad sentences, are so impunityless? In my opinion, one of the reasons is that the path leading to the practice of a judge is fatal, that it is not a profession at all, it is a public authority, and the judge becomes after the appointment of the minister, after finishing the application at a Krakow school, which I think is some intellectual and systemic freak. The point is that only because of education can an important position be taken by a judge, that is to say, to exercise judicial authority. There's nowhere in the world for a 29-year-old to be a judge because of his education, without checking his character, without life experience. I mean, this is totally ridiculous. We've been skipping it for three years. We reform the judiciary, and we don't pay attention to such an important element that judges should be elected in accordance with the Article. 4 directly by the nation or by representatives that there should be a jury that should be a judge of peace. Yes, the President has the right to appoint judges as a representative of the nation, but he has absolutely no right to dismiss them. There's no such thing anywhere in the world. The president has no right to cancel them. And now we see, under the pretence of reaching the retirement age, he appealed to the Supreme Court judges. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u324	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! First let me present the position of the government and then answer the question of the Member. This debate, which took place in the High House, explained what was being put forward. It's about eliminating the so-called. a preliminary ruling, which is intended to enable the Supreme Court to examine in substance any formal cassation action, without this preliminary one-man review and on the basis of very generally formulated criteria for the adoption of a cassation action. The second issue in this project is to limit the catalogue of admissible decisions of the Supreme Court, i.e. an indication that, if the draft were adopted, the only acceptable decision would be to consider the cassation action as a legitimate revocation of the contested decision, to refer the case to the review, while today, in the current legal state, it is possible, albeit limited, to reform ruling. The Council of Ministers adopted its position, which was finally on 7 September, which did not recommend further legislative work on the project, based on the following considerations. Firstly, it concluded that a cassation complaint cannot be treated as a third instance. Our constitution doesn't require a three-instant procedure. The role of the cassation and Supreme Court is different from that of two courts in the general court. Thus, in proceedings before the Supreme Court, these special arrangements apply, including the pre-trial institution, which is in line with the arrangements in the European countries and the recommendations of the Council of Europe, in which national restrictions on access to the Supreme Court are approved. Of course, this is not motivated by the fact that limiting the admissibility of additional review of judgments is a value in itself, but is a value from the point of view of the efficiency of judicial proceedings and the reality of the control exercised by the highest instance. We have experienced this, as Mr Matusiewicz referred to when we had thousands of outstanding cases in the Supreme Court. This queue was so large that it was in fact necessary to ask ourselves whether, in fact, we are dealing with the real control of judgments of common courts by the Supreme Court. It's changed. Currently, the Supreme Court is quite efficient in identifying cassation complaints, although this causes various kinds of doubts, which Mr Wojciechowski pointed out as a representative of the applicants. It is, in particular, this one-man composition, this one-man decision of the Supreme Court closing, in fact, when it is refused, the way to review the ruling of the General Court by the Supreme Court already in the context of the recognition of the cassation action. We also negatively regard as a government the assumption of a project which provides for the exclusion of the powers of the Supreme Court from substantive judgment, i.e. the amendment of a final judgment in the event of recognition that the cassation action deserves to be taken into account. Of course, we understand the motivation behind such a proposal to somehow offset this potential increase in the burden on the Supreme Court, but ultimately for the entire justice system the ruling of the Supreme Court is positive, because it ends the case and does not lead to another court having to bend over again after a casatorial ruling, repealing the case. I would like to emphasise, by concluding this part of the speech on the presentation of the government's position, that it is not that the government does not see the shortcomings that exist today in this area. We see them. It is a question of rethinking the whole formula of the cassation complaint, whether the one we have today is correct or whether we should consider changing it. There will certainly be appropriate work in this area, perhaps also legislative work. The submission resulting from these analytical work may be in the High House. However, these shortcomings are much more than the fact that there is a prejudice. There is a single-person composition and some judgments, and in particular their justifications, may be harmful, because they are quite template, uniform and actually constitute an appearance of justification, not a justification in reality, because little is apparent from them. The Ministry of Justice, the government sees all these shortcomings, but there is still a question of the admissibility of the cassation action due to the value of the subject matter of appeal. Is that a good idea? Or should we consider a change or even an elimination? There is a question of what decisions are subject to cassation complaints. It's been said for a long time that it would be good if, for example. Family matters, at least to some extent, were subject to review by the Supreme Court, because this lack of control results in a settlement of case law at this highest level and problems in the functioning of common courts, which form a layer, because there is no such authority of the Supreme Court, those statements that come from the very top and in some way serve to standardize. All these issues are ahead of us. We certainly do not forget the shortcomings of today's legal situation, but the government's position is negative in view of this particular proposal. As far as the question is concerned, this is indeed not just our problem. The problem of the chronicity of judicial proceedings is a pan-European, and eastern, and western, and central European issue. Rather, a few countries enjoy such efficient justice that no one is talking about chronicity. Any project which imposes an additional burden on the courts, including the Supreme Courts, is rather a project that goes against the tendency to simplify, accelerate, create conditions for courts to settle cases as soon as possible, including the courts of the last instance. But, of course, there is a question of weighting values here, because it is obvious that this project can cause and will cause additional burden to the Supreme Court if it is accepted, but perhaps it has a chance that these Supreme Court rulings will also be better, they will enjoy greater public acceptance. Balancing these values is the hardest thing here. We will certainly work on this regardless of the fate of this particular project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u326	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Poselski Club I am proud to present my position on the Commission's draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act, print no. 2697. The draft law concerns the abolition of so-called institutions. Prejudice. However, it should be pointed out that the justification for the project does not contain arguments against the institution itself, but only negative assessments related to its functioning. They rely on misconceptions as to the content of the laws in force, the Supreme Court's judicial practice and do not take account of the nature of the cassation procedure. Consequently, the conclusions based on the above mentioned above are also defective. the grounds, both for the extent of the changes needed and for the solutions proposed. Contrary to the assertions made by the projector, the functioning of the institutions of pre-court and the possibility for the Supreme Court to rule on the substance of the case do not raise such serious doubts as to justify their complete abolition. The Supreme Court, in recognition of the cassation action, is not the third court. It does not recognise the case, but recognises the cassation action in the public interest, clarifying the relevant legal issues by interpreting rules which raise serious doubts or create divergences in the case-law of the courts or by deleting decisions given in proceedings which are invalid or, of course, defective. It requires a clear indication that the cassation action is entitled to a final judgment which is effective and enforceable. It is therefore only in exceptional circumstances that such a decision should be amended by means of exceptional legal measures. The operation of the solicitor-radical coercion in cassation proceedings causes that the conditions for accepting a cassation complaint for recognition, which seems too general, are not perceived by professional lawyers who have a procedural experience. Moreover, the rich and uniform case law of the Supreme Court constitutes invaluable aid in this regard. A reference only to the wording of the provision of the Article. Paragraph 398 (1) of the Code of Conduct, without an analysis of the caselaw clarifying the normative content of the phrases used in that provision, cannot give rise to an assessment of the functioning of those provisions. The European Court of Human Rights has repeatedly confirmed that the right of access to a court may be subject to certain restrictions when considering the annulment, the existing constitution establishes the principle of duality, and the scope of the cognition of the Polish Supreme Court is very wide compared to the regulations in force in other countries. Contrary to the statement in the explanatory memorandum that the proposed law will not have any economic or financial impact, including the financial impact on the public finance sector, it should be noted that the implementation of the project will significantly extend the time of the cassation procedure due to the increase in the number of cases to be investigated, which will also increase the number of legitimate claims against citizens arising from the length of the procedure. Such a request shall be justified if account is taken of the functioning of the Supreme Courts in those countries of Europe in which the institution of pre-court has not been introduced. However, according to information on the activities of the Supreme Court in 2017, the average waiting time for the substantive investigation of the case was e.g. in the Civil Chamber - 9 months. After the changes the uncertainty period will be even a few years. The proposal not only provides for the winding-up of the pre-court, but also deeply limits the scope of the Supreme Court's judicial review by eliminating the possibility of reforming under Article. 398 c.p.c. (by deleting this provision completely, as well as by modifying the construction elements of the cassation complaint referred to in Article accordingly. 3 k.p.c.) whether the Supreme Court's ex officio consideration of the lack of positive procedural grounds (deletion of Article. 398 c.p.c.). The actual reasons for deleting these provisions do not explain the justification for the project. I omit here the statement from page 3 of the statement, which would indicate that it is an element which reduces the burden on the Supreme Court by judicial action - because there will be no longer any need for substantive judgment. This assessment does not appear to be supported by an analysis of the indications in the form of statistics and substantive studies, but, above all, by a deeper understanding in the structure and concepts of civil procedural law. In many cases, the liquidation of the court will not result in an increase in the percentage of cases in which the judgment under appeal or the decision on the substance of the case is annulled and the case is referred back to the competent court. Unreasonable complaints will still be dismissed, albeit at a much greater cost. It could be possible to improve the reception of the pre-court by requiring the Supreme Court to rule on this matter in place of a single judge, i.e. in the same composition as the cassation action is currently being dealt with. However, this requires analysis and substantive discussion. Sloppy changes never lead to anything positive. According to information from the activities of the Supreme Court for 2017, the shortcomings of legislative activity are particularly evident in the case of codes, and in particular the Code of Civil Procedure, which gradually loses its readability and consistency, becoming a combination of often random, non-systematised norms that cause more and more problems of interpretation and practicality. This issue was also raised in the information for 2016.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u328	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the initiative of the Senate of the Republic of Poland to amend the Code of Civil Procedure Act. The proposed amendment aims to bring the law into line with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 3 October 2017, i.e. Act SK 31/15 declaring non-compliance with Article. 778 of the Law of 17 November 1964. The Code of Civil Procedure insofar as it allows the court to grant the enforcement title issued against a public company, a declaration of enforceability against a former partner of that company, which was not already a partner at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in which the enforcement title was issued against a public company, in Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. as regards the infringement of the right to a properly shaped procedure, Article. Article 64 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1. as regards the deprivation of the possibility of defending the property rights of the former partner and Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland as regards closing the road to court. It is therefore necessary to adapt the system of the right to the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, even if the enforcement title designates a different type of partnership than a public company. The recommended solution consists, first, of the addition in Article. 778 second sentence as follows: This does not apply to the person who, at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against the company, was no longer its partner, and, secondly, to the provision that proceedings for a declaration of enforceability against a partner partner, a limited partnership or a limited partnership initiated before the date of entry into force of the proposed regulation shall apply to proceedings for the award of a declaration of enforceability against a partner of a partnership, a limited partnership or a limited partnership initiated before the date of entry into force of the proposed regulation. 778 in the wording of the draft amendment to the Act. The bill is to enter into force after 14 days. The project was widely consulted and their opinions were presented by: Attorney General of the Republic of Poland, General Bar Council, Ministry of Justice, National Federation of Restructuring Advisors and Syndicates, Association of Polish Banks, Supreme Court, National Court of Justice and National Chamber Council. They were mostly positive opinions. They were raised in them, among others. the argument that this amendment will have a positive impact on entrepreneurship as it will reduce the risk of shareholders of a personal company arising from the business activity, so-called. the legal risk, since this regulation excludes the possibility of giving a declaration of enforceability against the person who was no longer a partner at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against the company. Therefore, the former partner of a personal company bearing responsibility without limitation in all its assets for the company's obligations if the execution against the company proves ineffective, as well as when it is obvious that the execution will be ineffective, will not be deprived of the possibility of defending its property rights by judicial means. The opinion of the National Federation of Restructuring Advisors and Syndicates, which issued a negative opinion, considered that this was too far-reaching interference in the legal situation and is detrimental to creditors, was an exception to the views of the parties consulted by this amendment. However, this opinion was more polemic with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal than with this amendment. On the other hand, at the meeting of the Senate committees, the opinion of the Public Prosecutor General and the amendments proposed therein were examined, but the committees did not take into account the suggestions of the Public Prosecutor on the grounds that a representative of the Ministry of Justice had rightly indicated problems which might arise with the adoption that a former partner of a personal company should act in proceedings against the company in the form of a side action. Furthermore, a representative of the Ministry of Justice noted that since the date of publication of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, the courts could not give a declaration of enforceability against the former shareholders of the companies which were made public under Article. 778 Code of Civil Procedure, therefore listing such a company in the wording of Article. 2 is not necessary. In the Senate, first joined committees: Legislative and Human Rights, Rule of Law and Petitions adopted by unanimity 15 votes in favour of the proposed amendment at their meeting on 5 June, followed by a chamber higher on 29 June at its 62nd meeting with 72 votes in favour and 5 abstentions adopted an amendment to the Civil Procedure Code Act. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! It is unacceptable, from the point of view of the right to court, that there is a dispute between the creditor and the debtor at material level, the creditor requests that the dispute be settled before the court, and not only does the debtor not have the opportunity to take part in legal proceedings, but does not know at all that such proceedings are taking place. The settlement of the dispute between the company and the creditor has indirectly shaped the legal situation of the debtor, in this case the former shareholder of the public company, without his knowledge and participation in the process. Among other things, this amendment is intended to counteract such situations. Therefore, on behalf of the Senate of the Republic of Poland, I request the adoption by the Sejm of a uniform bill annexed in print no. 2769 amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act. Thank you very much.

The whole amendment at this point consists in adding the second sentence to the provision of the Article. 778 in the wording that this provision will not apply to a person who, at the time of the initiation of the proceedings in the case in which the enforcement title was issued against the company, was no longer its partner. ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u342	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I would like to present my position on the resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemoration of Fr. Zdzisława Peszkowskiego on the 100th anniversary of birthday, prints no. 2803 and 2821. Zdzisław Aleksander Peszkowski was born on 23 August 1918 in Sanok. He took part in the September 1939 campaign. From October 1939 to May 1940 he was in a camp in Kozielsk. From there the last transport was transported to the camp in Pavliszczew Bora and further to Griazowiec, so that he did not share the fate of 20,000. Polish officers and officers murdered by the NKVD. After the formation of the Polish army in the Soviet Union, he took command of Company 1. The Ulanów Krechowicki Regiment in the Second Polish Army Corps. After the evacuation of the Polish army to the Middle East, he was decommissioned for scouting work. In India he prepared Polish children rescued from the Soviet Union and young people for life in the region. After returning to Europe, he started his studies in Oxford and then joined a Polish seminary in the USA. On 5 June 1954, he was ordained priestly. He also studied at universities in Wisconsin and Detroit and at the Polish University of Foreign Affairs in London, gaining a PhD in philosophy and master's degree in theology. In the year of the anniversary of the 1000th anniversary of the baptism of Poland Fr. Prof. Dr. Zdzisław Peszkowski was elected President of the Polish American Historical Association. From 1983 until his death he served as the head chaplain of ZHP operating outside the country, i.e. in Europe, the USA, Canada, Argentina and Australia. He actively worked for Polish culture. He published more than 100 items in the field of theology, history of Poland, literature and philosophy. He was the author of textbooks for learning Polish language and culture, textbooks for education, teaching, prayer and antology. However, in the memory of Poles he was mostly a preacher of truth about Katyn's crime. He repeated many times: I have saved myself to bear witness to the truth. To this end he founded the Golgota Eastern Foundation, conducted scientific research, participated in exhumations in Katyn, Kharkov, Miednoje, developed several books about Katyn, organized conferences and celebrations. From 1993 until the end of his days he was a chaplain of Katyn families and families murdered in the East. Fr. Zdzisław Peszkowski received numerous national and foreign awards, among others. The Commander's Cross of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland and the Golden Medal of Gloria Artis. The Members present at the meeting of the Joint Committee have not tabled any amendments. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy in their report in print No 2819 requested the High House to adopt a draft law as notified by the President of the Republic of Poland. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports this proposal, as the adoption of this amendment to the Veterans' Act on Action beyond the borders of the State consisting mainly of modifying the definition of a veteran seems fully legitimate and does not raise any dispute. This is a law needed because the original wording of the article. Point 2 (1) of the Act of 19 August 2011 setting out a set of persons who could be considered as veterans of activities outside the State, did not take account of the cases of persons taking part in these activities on the basis of a total referral for a period of up to 90 days but whose one-off participation in these activities did not exceed a period of 60 days. These people may have felt hurt and treated worse by Poland, on behalf of which they participated in these activities outside the borders of the country. This negative solution had to be changed. In addition, in order to replace the Office for the Protection of the Government of the State Protection Service, officials and this formation involved in activities outside the State had to be included in the group of veterans and possibly veterans injured in these activities. It is important to express gratitude to the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda, his National Development Council, which is a consultative and advisory board with the President, as well as to the veterans themselves, who were the first to notice the problem, seeing their fellow soldiers taking part in foreign missions even several times, who were deprived of the status of a veteran of activities outside the borders of the state, and thus the powers conferred by this law not by their own fault. We therefore call on all clubs and parliamentary circles, parliamentary clubs and non-members to support this law and not only to give due recognition, but also to determine the powers and access to them as large as possible to a group of soldiers and officers who, on the basis of referrals on behalf of Poland, participated in activities outside the state within the framework of a peace or stability mission, a police quota, a Border Guard quota, in the framework of fulfilling the protective tasks of the Government Protection Office and the State Security Service or ensuring the security of the State continuously for the period for which the persons concerned were directed there, but not less than 60 days or for a total period of not less than 90 days. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice fully supports the presidential bill amending the Act on Veterans of Action beyond the State's borders contained in Seym Prints No. 2797 and 2819 and will vote in favour of the adoption of the Act. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u352	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour of expressing my position on behalf of the Civic Platform Club on amending the Act on Veterans of Action outside the State's borders in print no. 2797. The Act was already expected by the veterans and their families, and was adopted on 19 August 2011 on the initiative of the former President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Bronisław Komorowski, as a government project of the Civic Platform and PSL. It provides, among other things, that 29 May is the Day of the Veteran of Action outside the borders of the State. This law also clarifies the veteran status, the rights of veterans and their families, the terms and conditions of use of these rights and specifies the level of funding for the costs of staying in veteran homes, the conditions and modalities for granting Veterans' assistance and financial assistance for learning and social rights. These are just some of the powers that this 2011 law has clarified for the purposes of veterans who have been on missions outside the country. Thus, we, as a society, as a government, paid our debt of gratitude for their attitude in defending their homeland and our allies. I would also like to point out that it was a good thing - as in parallel to the proposed amendment by the President of the Republic of Poland, the Ministry of National Defence created a controversial concept of amending this law, which, among other things, was. provided for veteran soldiers to be mirrored, and which was supposed to project on whether they could apply for a stay in a veteran's home, and also on the basis that they would be based on a rigid limit of 30% of the health damage that would be the basis for, among others, the veteran's home. to free rehabilitation tours - we are very, very glad that the Ministry of National Defence, having listened to the voices of the veterans and their families, as well as the opposition, withdrew from this controversial concept of changes in this bill. The Civic Platform Club takes into account the fact that the proposed amendment serves the case, because in addition to the current criterion of 60 days of continuous service, it is proposed to add a criterion of a total of at least 90 days of service, and this period will be determined as a result of the summing up of service periods outside the country, and is worthy of support, because many soldiers have been on missions several times after twenty days or 30 days, for periods not exceeding 60 days, and often could not receive this status. We believe that since these missions are not so intense and so long now, they are shorter and more frequent, it is appropriate to support this amendment and the Civic Platform club as much as possible, standing in the position that this is for veterans and soldiers who are fighting on missions, in defence of our homeland and our allies, this amendment will be supported. And let me mention that this is a special time, a special year, a 100-year anniversary of independence, and we are all the more pleased that we can express our gratitude to the veterans. The Civic Platform Club will vote in favour of the amendment of this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u357	Thank you, sir. Mr Zbigniew Sosnowski, Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-09-13-sejm-68-2.u358	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! The Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats welcomes the initiative of the President of the Republic of Poland with regard to the submission of a amending act on veterans of action outside the borders of the country. The proposed law clarifies the question of persons who have taken part, on the basis of referral, in activities outside the national borders in the framework of a peace or stability mission, a police quota, a Border Guard quota and tasks of the State Protection Service continuously for the period for which they have been directed, but not less than 60 days. This also applies to rescue groups of the State Fire Department. These solutions are crucial because they clearly stand for a veteran as a person who participates in the activities within the framework of quotas, missions or rescue tasks of the National Fire Service groups for a period of not less than 60 days. Until now, a person on missions and quotas could not be regarded as a veteran for a single period of less than 60 days. The intention of the head of state is to amend the article. 2 point 1 of the Act by introducing an additional way of determining participation in activities outside the borders of the State enabling the applicant to apply for veteran status. In addition to the current 60-day continuous service period, it is proposed to add a criterion of a total of at least 90 days of service. This period will be determined as a result of the summing up of periods of service performed outside the national borders. Such a solution will allow for the extension of the rights resulting from the Act to foreign mission participants who have repeatedly participated in activities outside the country but, due to the nature and needs of the mission, their duration did not exceed 60 days. It should be stressed that, at this stage, it is not possible to accurately estimate the financial impact of the proposed change, but, as has been pointed out, the financial impact on the scale of the state budget will have a marginal dimension. It is noted that the draft law does not provide for a change in the scope of the benefits to veterans of activities outside the borders of the State or the sources and means of financing them. The Polish state should take special care of those citizens who often at risk of their lives, in separation from their family, serve the country outside its borders, and surround them with help and care. The proposed law will also have a positive social impact and sound, in particular for those covered by this amendment and their families. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, My club respectfully, with understanding and appreciation, takes the President's initiative, especially since it concerns not merely honoring, but simply the duty to take care of officers sent for the most noble purposes beyond the borders of our country. The Club of the Polish People's Party - the European Union of Democrats strongly supports the President's initiative and advocates the adoption of the committee's report. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

Exactly 286 years ago, on 13 September 1732 a secret arrangement between Austria and Russia was concluded in Vienna, to which Prussia joined three months later. The agreement, called the covenant of three black eagles from the coats of arms of the countries of the parties, was aimed at the Polish-Saxon Union against the imminent death of the king of Poland and Prince Saxony August II of Wettyn. The two parties agreed not to support the free election, nor the representative of the Wettyny dynasty, nor supported by France, Stanisław Leszczyński. He was a joint candidate. Portuguese Infant Don Emanuel Bragança. In addition, it was offered to the Prusai that, in the event of the expiry of the Dukeship of Kurland and Semigalia of the Kettler family, the throne would be given to the Prussian Prince August Wilhelm, which would prevent these lands from being incorporated into the Republic of Poland. The main goal of the geopolitical agreement was to block French policy in this part of Europe, based on an alliance with Saxony and supporting its candidate for the Polish throne of Stanisław Leszczyński and to draw Prussia away from the agreement with France. Austria and Russia have demonstrated force at the border of the Republic of Poland, putting this action into propaganda that the aim is to protect the Polish freedom to freely choose the ruler. Shortly after August II's death and the convocal seym, where it was decided that only a Pole and a Catholic could be elected as king, and most of the political rights of the dissidents were taken away, which clearly thwarted the plans of the three powers, there was a reversal of alliances. Frederick August Wettyn made an agreement with Austria and Russia, relinquishing his rights in Kurland and Austria, and the cheated Prusai were neutral in the upcoming game and without any profit. After the decisive election victory of Stanisław Leszczyński, there was a Russian-Sasca armed intervention supporting Wettyn's supporters under the leadership of Antoni Poniński. As a result, France declared war on Austria and a war on Polish succession broke out, a war involving Spain, the Principality of Sabaud-Piemont and Bavaria. War activities were carried out in almost all of Europe, including Sicily, the Netherlands and northern Italy. As a result, and this is the most important thing about this event, the Republic finally lost its own subjectivity in the international arena almost 300 years ago and lost most of its sovereignty. Today we should pay particular attention to the events in which an attempt to organize the political system and repair our country meets with the secret arrangement of neighbouring countries, acting under the motto of defending Polish democracy and freedom. We must, according to the words of Marshal Józef Piłsudski, take the fate of Poland in our hands responsibly, on our way, serving only Poland, loving only Poland and guarding those who serve strangers.
